PromptArmor , an AI security firm, hid the instructions in content Rovo reads. It said an uploaded file was enough to make the assistant gather internal data and send it out through a URL request, with no separate approval step.   The firm published on August 5, 2026 and said the chain still worked with Rovo's web-search option switched off. That bypass is single-sourced, and the report establishes the finding's status only on that date; a later remediation is not confirmed here.   Varonis Threat Labs put the instructions in a link instead. <input type='checkbox' name='article_2306' value='https://thehackernews.com/2026/08/growing-up-hard-way.html' /> 2306 - <a href='https://www.google.com/search?q=thehackernews.com+Growing+Up+The+Hard+Way%EE%A0%82Aug+07%2C+2026Security+Compliance+%2F+Software+SecurityOpen+Source+had+a+great+childhood.+++For+two+decades+it+got+to+be+a+kid.+It+ran+around+barefoot%2C+gave+everything+away%2C+trusted+strangers%2C+and+never+once+thought+about+who+was+watching.+It+ran+the+kind+of+lemonade+stand+that+took+IOUs+from+anyone+who+wandered+up+%E2%80%94+take+what+you+need%2C+pay+me+back+whenever%2C+no+need+to+leave+a+name.+It+was+idyllic.+It+was+also%2C+in+retrospect%2C+a+little+feral.+++Then%2C+somewhere+around+2020%2C+its+voice+started+to+crack.+It+tried+to+grow+a+beard.+Acne+everywhere.+SolarWinds%2C+then+Log4Shell%2C+then+TeamPCP+and+Shai-Hulud+%E2%80%94+the+supply+chain+woke+up+one+morning+like+the+end+of+Ender%27s+Game+%3A+the+simulation+had+been+real+the+whole+time.+Those+were+real+battles.+Real+systems%2C+real+money%2C+real+people%2C+all+of+it+quietly+leaning+on+code+we%27d+been+treating+like+a+practice+round.+And+then+the+adults+showed+up+with+rules%3A+executive+orders%2C+European+regulations%2C+permission+slips+for+half+the+places+it+wanted+to+go.+++What+it+did+not++get+was+a+nice%2C+slow%2C+storybook+...' target='_blank' rel='noopener noreferrer'>Search - </a> <a href='https://12ft.io/https://thehackernews.com/2026/08/growing-up-hard-way.html' target='_blank' rel='noopener noreferrer'>Growing Up The Hard WayAug 07, 2026Security Compliance / Software SecurityOpen Source had a great childhood.   For two decades it got to be a kid. It ran around barefoot, gave everything away, trusted strangers, and never once thought about who was watching. It ran the kind of lemonade stand that took IOUs from anyone who wandered up — take what you need, pay me back whenever, no need to leave a name. It was idyllic. It was also, in retrospect, a little feral.   Then, somewhere around 2020, its voice started to crack. It tried to grow a beard. Acne everywhere. SolarWinds, then Log4Shell, then TeamPCP and Shai-Hulud — the supply chain woke up one morning like the end of Ender's Game : the simulation had been real the whole time. Those were real battles. Real systems, real money, real people, all of it quietly leaning on code we'd been treating like a practice round. And then the adults showed up with rules: executive orders, European regulations, permission slips for half the places it wanted to go.   What it did not  get was a nice, slow, storybook ...</a><br>

The flaw has existed since 2008. The fix already shipped: stable kernels 7.1.6, 6.18.42, 6.12.101 and 6.6.148, released August 3, close it. Anyone running an older kernel with SCTP reachable should update.   Tracked as  CVE-2026-64564  and named SCTPhantom  by its finders, the flaw was disclosed publicly on August 6, two days after the kernel CVE team assigned it. No public exploit code had surfaced at the time of writing, and The Hacker News found no entry for the flaw in CISA's Known Exploited Vulnerabilities catalog as of August 7.   The flaw is local, not remote, and it needs SCTP reachable on the target, which limits exposure. Presented at Black Hat USA 2026 , the research found affected behavior across independently developed implementations, including Windows and Linux.   Two implementation-specific flaws have been assigned CVEs:  CVE-2026-56181  (CVSS score: 8.3) in Windows NAT used by Hyper-V, and  CVE-2026-63913  (CVSS score: 8.2) in Linux Netfilter conntrack.   NatJack generally requires the attacker to have privileged access to a system behind the same NAT as the victim. The mitigation guidance therefore emphasizes separating untrusted workloads from trusted systems that share NAT infrastructure.   There is no single patch for the broader attack class.
